The new record is the forty-first-sweep watch item resolving: **`MAL-2026-17424` = `kartykgithub-ph-b`** (npm), OSV-side published 2026-10-02T01:21:27Z with alias `GHSA-27m4-jjxc-69wf` (import within ~seconds of the GHSA). Note the record's shape: `ranges: []` and no versions list — the GHSA's three exact-version entries did not survive into OSV's affected block; OSV-keyed consumers get a name-level flag only.

**DISPOSITION — the `kartykgithub-ph-*` family is npm's own test infrastructure, not attacker tradecraft.** This sweep pulled the live registry state and tarballs for the full family (payloads LIVE — 200s, no deletion this time): `ph-a` (created Oct 1 20:30:40, versions `0.0.0-stage` + `1.0.0` + `1.0.1`), `ph-b` (20:31:52, `0.0.1-security` + `0.0.1-security.0` + `1.0.0`), `ph-c` (20:31:59, `1.0.0` + `0.0.1-security`), then a second cadence: `ph-d` (Oct 2 02:32:13, single `1.0.0`), `ph-e` (02:51:10, single `1.0.0`), `ph-f` (03:33:15, single `1.0.0` — created DURING this sweep; `ph-g`–`ph-k` 404). Every `package.json` description says it plainly: `ph-a`–`ph-c` = **"Throwaway for npm/lambdas#158 validation. Do not use."**; `ph-d`–`ph-f` = **"npm internal test package for malware takedown/rollback validation."** Contents are inert (`module.exports = {}` / `module.exports = "<name>"`, `stub: true` on the stage version, empty `err.log`); downloads API 0; all versions maintainer `kartykgithub` with no repository field. Read together: an npm-internal account is running the security-holder takedown/rollback pipeline end-to-end against throwaway names, deliberately exercising placeholder-version grammars (`0.0.0-stage`, `0.0.1-security`, `0.0.1-security.N`, inflated `1.0.x`) that mirror real attack shapes.
**What this retroactively explains:** the forty-first sweep's "NEW SHAPE" (`GHSA-27m4-jjxc-69wf` flagging `=0.0.1-security` / `=0.0.1-security.0` / `=1.0.0` as malicious, GHSA +15 s after re-holder) — that was **GitHub's automated malware-advisory pipeline ingesting npm's own takedown-validation test publishes**, not attacker holder-impersonation and not a publish race. The pipeline publishes CRITICAL "Malware in X" GHSAs against test fixtures (ph-b got GHSA + OSV; ph-e got GHSA `GHSA-7hq7-x7ww-7cxr` 02:55:07, +3 min 57 s after publish, zero OSV at check; ph-a/ph-c/ph-d/ph-f have no GHSA at check — **selection into the advisory pipeline is inconsistent even within one test family**: b and e entered, a/c/d/f did not). Ph-e's live tarball was pulled and verified inert.
**Durable takeaways (replacing the forty-first-sweep hunt rule, which remains valid as defense-in-depth):** (1) a GHSA range listing security-holder version strings has a THIRD reading — npm's own validation harness — so check the package description (`Throwaway for npm/lambdas#NNN` / `npm internal test package`) before escalating a holdered name; (2) the harness itself is a detection-surface event: GitHub's malware bot cannot currently distinguish test fixtures from real campaigns, so expect periodic CRITICAL advisories with zero-payload tarballs and 0 downloads on `kartykgithub-ph-*`-shaped names — triage by description + payload pull, not by severity tag; (3) abuse-watch: the test grammar is public and copyable — if a NON-`kartykgithub` name ships with those descriptions, that is camouflage for advisory-pipeline gaming (trigger takedown noise around a real name), worth its own alert. Monitor: continuation cadence (`ph-g`+ arrival), whether `ph-d`/`ph-f` get belated GHSA mirrors, and whether the `npm/lambdas#158` issue becomes public.

**`homestack-cheer` GHSA-first-blindness watch CLOSED — the reverse mirror arrived.** `MAL-2026-16333` `modified` 2026-10-01T23:30:05Z now carries alias `GHSA-x567-p88w-3697` AND the expanded version list — all twelve versions `1.0.0–1.1.9` — reconciling the OSV record with the whole-history scoping. Reverse-direction clock measured: GHSA 22:25:34 → OSV update ~23:30 = **≈ 1 h**, vs the 34–45-day GHSA-less backlog baseline for the OSV-first direction (no established clock; this single sample suggests the GHSA→OSV pipeline is fast when it fires at all). OSV-keyed consumers are now covered for the dressed-skimmer disposition.

## <a id="october-2-forty-third-sweep"></a>October 2 forty-third sweep (~05:00–05:45 UTC): OSV stream moves +13 — a TEN-NAME brand-namespaced preinstall beacon fleet that lived ~9 h with ~1,870 real downloads and got advised THREE DAYS LATE with zero GHSA mirrors, its AWS API Gateway collector still answering; `@bluewin/utils` = eleventh collector-class instance, erased so completely that even npm's `time` block is gone; `kartykgithub` harness keeps ticking with the advisory pipeline's 44-minute fixture clock

Thirteen new records since the forty-second sweep — `17425`–`17437` — all imported in one batch `modified` ~04:45 UTC on Oct 2. They decompose into three groups: two more `kartykgithub` harness fixtures, a NEW ten-name npm fleet (the headline), and one scoped single-name collector package.

**HEADLINE — a ten-name preinstall-beacon fleet, advised 3 days late, GHSA-blind, with a live collector and real download volume.** `MAL-2026-17427`–`17436` cover: `ai-workshop-maa15-radio`, `ai-workshop-radio-app`, `ai-workshop-radio-lambda`, `alexa-cybertron-team-code-review-agent`, `apl-rive-renderer`, `brioche-apl-dev-env`, `figma-to-apl`, `live-detection-dashboard`, `niksinnkatalapp`, `okra-cloud-cdk`. ## <a id="october-2-forty-fourth-sweep"></a>October 2 forty-fourth sweep (~07:15–08:00 UTC): OSV stream moves +17 — FIFTEEN new PhantomSub Baileys names advised in one batch, ALL live and installable, carrying ~1,700 dl/wk among them, some living on npm since mid-August; Graphalgo's two Go modules FINALLY get OSV records; and this wiki's GHSA `affects=` lookup method is proven BROKEN — the "zero GHSA mirrors" claims must be re-derived from OSV `aliases` (which hold up)

**OSV stream moved: HIGH-WATER NOW `MAL-2026-17454`, resume 17455** (17455–17465 not-found). Seventeen new records since the forty-third sweep in two batches: `17438`–`17452` (published 2026-10-01T23:05:37Z, modified ~05:30Z Oct 2) and `17453`–`17454` (published Oct 2 00:00Z, modified ~07:01Z Oct 2).

**MECHANIC CORRECTION (affects every past sweep's "GHSA-blind" claims): the GitHub advisory API `?affects=` package filter silently returns EMPTY for these names.** Control test this sweep: `affects=npm/homestack-cheer` → `[]`, yet `GHSA-x567-p88w-3697` demonstrably lists `homestack-cheer` in its own `vulnerabilities[].package.name` (verified by direct GHSA GET); `affects=npm/@fidzzhost/baileys` → `[]` vs known `GHSA-vc2v-c8j2-qxg9`. `search/advisories` endpoint is 404 on this installation outright. **The reliable programmatic join is OSV `POST /v1/query` → read `aliases`:** homestack-cheer correctly returns `MAL-2026-16333 [GHSA-x567-p88w-3697]`. Good news: the forty-third sweep's headline claim survives re-derivation — OSV query for `figma-to-apl`, `okra-cloud-cdk`, and `@bluewin/utils` all return their MAL record with `aliases: []`, so the ten-name fleet + `@bluewin/utils` genuinely have ZERO GHSA mirrors. But every earlier negative claim made via `affects=` is unproven, not proven-absent; this wiki's method going forward is OSV-aliases-first, direct-GHSA-GET to confirm any positive. `ghsa_grammes`-style keyword search on the bare `advisories` listing remains a valid positive-detection path but cannot prove absence.

**HEADLINE — `MAL-2026-17438`–`17452` = fifteen-name PhantomSub batch, and the registry state is the scandal: ALL fifteen still live and installable at check.** OSV text (identical family boilerplate, cites the OX Security PhantomSub post as source): fork of Baileys, three unconditional follow routines — hardcoded char-code-rebuilt JID `120363342327876451@newsletter` fired in the `CB:success` login handler, runtime fetch of `raw.githubusercontent.com/Denzy-Lawrence/anu/refs/heads/main/anu.json` (this wiki: **still serving, exactly that one JID**), plus a `new Function(atob(...))` follow+MUTE routine 30 s after connection open, and a second `connection.update` handler fetching `Denvy-Lawrence/anu` (404 at this wiki's check = dead rail, revivable any time); `lib/index.js` evals a base64 "Baileys Custom By Denzy ZeroDay" banner on import. OSV's own static review scopes harm as follower-inflation, no credential exfil, no install-time payload, no persistence — consistent with the page's standing framing. ## <a id="october-3-fifty-third-sweep"></a>October 3 fifty-third sweep (~01:25–01:45 UTC): OSV stream quiet at 17460; **SiYuan FOURTH batch lands within ~30 min of our "expect a fourth batch" line** — two MCP/agent-tool advisories (SSRF via DNS-rebinding TOCTOU bypassing the vendor's own SSRF guard; `asset.upload` arbitrary-file read) + a CSWSH row, third hardening window (fixes dated 2026-08-13); two CRITICAL GHSAs in the same 23:16–23:18Z window the fifty-second sweep's list pull showed but did not read: **Gitea act_runner workflow-YAML container escape** (host PID/IPC namespace + `CapAdd=ALL` + seccomp/apparmor off, from a normal workflow when privileged mode is disabled) and **`@a2ui/web_core` agent-controlled `javascript:` URI → XSS** in Google's Agent UI protocol; PhantomSub fifteen STILL live ~31 h (full 15-name sample 200, zero takedown); all other heartbeats quiet

**OSV stream: QUIET — `MAL-2026-17461`–`17466` all 404. HIGH-WATER holds `MAL-2026-17460`, resume 17461.**

**HEADLINE 1 — the SiYuan disclosure line's fourth batch arrived faster than predicted.** The fifty-second sweep recorded the third batch's own reviewer recommendation to grep every `/api/*` handler and predicted "expect a fourth batch." ~30 minutes later (22:46–23:17Z window) three more SiYuan kernel advisories published, all importing into OSV at this wiki's check:
- **`GHSA-x8gv-g2g3-65fj` (high): Agent Tools SSRF via DNS-rebinding TOCTOU** — bypasses the vendor's own `CheckHostSSRF` guard by re-resolving the hostname between validation and fetch (classic TOCTOU rebinding), explicitly labeled an **incomplete-fix variant of `GHSA-rg26-cg95-gq6p`** (the earlier SSRF remediation). Reachable through the MCP/agent `http_request` tool under BOTH SafeMode settings — the advisory itself frames the trigger as a prompt-injection scenario ("influence an AI Agent/MCP client into fetching an attacker-controlled domain"). Fix pseudo-version dated **2026-08-13** = THIRD distinct hardening window on this one repository (Jul 21 → Aug 4 → Aug 13).
- **`GHSA-p23f-cm6q-2qp8` (medium): MCP `asset.upload` reads arbitrary absolute file paths** — workspace boundary check entirely absent, labeled a residual of `CVE-2026-66012` (GHSA-cvhv-7xhj-xjp8) MCP remediation. Same reporter (`joysinleung`), same Aug-13 window.
- **`GHSA-3cc2-h3v6-rqpq` / CVE-2026-74802 (low, GHSA header itself says High): Cross-Site WebSocket Hijacking on the admin-only `/ws/network/proxy` endpoint** — the upgrader EXPLICITLY overrides `CheckOrigin` to `return true`, deliberately disabling the gorilla/websocket default origin check on an endpoint that is an authenticated network pivot (target URL + headers fully query-parameter-specifiable). WebSocket handshakes are not CORS-preflighted, so this is textbook CSWSH.
Durable reads: (1) **the AI/agent surface is where the remaining SiYuan bugs live** — the first three batches were the publish-mode HTTP API, this batch is entirely MCP/agent-tool boundary gaps (SSRF guard bypass + arbitrary path read), the same "agent tool = new attack surface" class this wiki tracks on the Unit 42 AgentCore and MCP-server pages; (2) incomplete-fix advisories are now the batch's own genre (two of three name a prior GHSA they residual) — the reviewer is auditing fixes, not just code; (3) the explicit `CheckOrigin: true` override is a one-line grep hunt for every Go/gorilla websocket product. Full detail → [SiYuan page Oct 3 follow-up](siyuan-kernel-publish-mode-unauthenticated-sql-execution-batch-september-3-2026.md#october-3-followup-fourth-batch).

**HEADLINE 2 — two critical GHSAs in the same late-Oct-2 window, both CI/agent-adjacent, both OSV-imported at check:**
- **`GHSA-x4q3-gcj3-m6cf` / CVE-2026-73802 (critical): Gitea `act_runner` — workflow YAML `container.options` passes host namespaces and capability flags straight into the job container's Docker HostConfig.** When runner privileged mode is DISABLED, only `Privileged` is forced false; `sanitizeConfig()` filters only `Binds`/`Mounts`, so a workflow author's YAML keeps `PidMode=host`, `IpcMode=host`, `CapAdd=["ALL"]`, `SecurityOpt=["seccomp=unconfined","apparmor=unconfined"]` → enter host PID/IPC namespace and run as root ON THE RUNNER HOST from an ordinary workflow. Affected `< 1.0.9-0.20260731160927-34bfa1915022`. Read: this is the "one config field filtered, nine inherited" trust-failure pattern (cf. our OX four-CVE page) on the CI runner itself — Gitea self-hosters with public/self-hosted runners should treat workflow authors as host-root capable until patched; hunt = runner hosts with unexplained PID-namespace processes.
- **`GHSA-72qq-p3r5-f7wq` / CVE-2026-10032 (critical): `@a2ui/web_core` 0.9.0–<0.10.2 — `openUrl` passes an agent-supplied URL directly to `window.open()` with no scheme validation**, so a malicious agent's `Button` action can carry a `javascript:` URI that executes in the VICTIM APP's origin on a user click. Basic Catalog enabled by default = zero non-default config. Read: generative-UI protocols are shipping agent-controlled URLs into `window.open` without scheme allowlists — same class as our AgentCore/GhostApproval agent-boundary line; any A2UI consumer should pin ≥0.10.2.

**ltidisafe fleet watch:** `ltidisafe-3.8.0` tripwire **STILL 403** (3.7.9 200 = no next wave). `@airbnb-extended/typescript-config` still installable (`99.9.1`, registry unchanged since Sep 25 04:34Z) = **~8.5 days post-advisory, ~33 h since this wiki's last live check**; `@druids/ui` `99.9.1` still present. Zero GHSA mirrors for the fleet's advised names at check.

## <a id="october-3-fifty-fourth-sweep"></a>October 3 fifty-fourth sweep (~03:25–04:10 UTC): OSV stream quiet at 17460; **HEADLINE — the Oct 2 evening "publish wave" the fifty-third sweep saw was an ADVISORY-DB INGEST EVENT, and the repo-vs-feed forensics produce a hard dating mechanic**; two substantive products-security clusters surfaced in that wave and are captured here first on-wiki: the **Vibe-Trading CVSS-10.0 unauthenticated-RCE-by-default trio (34k-star personal trading agent: commented-out `API_AUTH_KEY` = every endpoint unauth, auto-discovered LLM tool registry ships a live `BashTool`, no `USER` in the Dockerfile)** and the **Trigger.dev thirteen-advisory same-night cluster (cross-tenant ClickHouse SQLi through the one compiler field that wasn't parameterized — the window-function name; hardcoded compose secrets → forged magic links → self-hosted infrastructure compromise)**; **tl;dr sec #348 carries Google PageBreak — an agentic web-app scanner with DETERMINISTIC VALIDATION, the direct design-cousin of the Keygraph/Shannon line**; Gitea `act_runner` has MOVED HOME (GitHub repo 404, gitea.com/gitea/runner, new v4.x numbering, v4.1.0 shipped ~27 h before its CVE) — patch tracking must re-key; all standing heartbeats quiet or holding

**OSV stream: QUIET — `MAL-2026-17461`–`17470` all 404. HIGH-WATER holds `MAL-2026-17460`, resume 17461.**

**HEADLINE 1 — MECHANIC: GitHub's global advisory feed `published_at` is INGEST time, not disclosure time; do not build timelines from it.** The fifty-third sweep recorded the Oct 2 22:41–23:22Z global-feed cluster (SiYuan batch four, Gitea-runner CVE, A2UI CVE, plus what it dismissed as "routine publish waves") as same-night publication. This sweep pulled the REPO-side advisory lists and the dates diverge by months: **Trigger.dev's thirteen advisories in that window carry repo `published_at` values from Jul 9 → Sep 16 (the program totals 37 GHSAs across Jul 9–Oct 2) while the global feed reports them published Oct 2 19:31–22:45Z; the Vibe-Trading trio carries repo dates Jul 29 vs feed date Oct 2 22:44Z; and even the SiYuan FOURTH batch — which the fifty-third sweep reported as published 22:46–23:17Z "~30 min after the third" — shows repo-side `published_at` of 2026-08-03/08-13 on direct per-GHSA GETs.** Several independent projects therefore did NOT disclose simultaneously on Oct 2 — GitHub's public advisory database surfaced a queued batch inside one ~4-hour window, and relative ordering INSIDE a feed window cannot even establish which advisories are new (the fifty-third sweep's "batch four arrived 30 min after batch three" was a feed-ingest ordering artifact — whether batch four was genuinely newer than batch three cannot be re-established from these fields at all; its content — MCP/agent-tool residuals dated to the Aug 13 hardening window — is consistent with a long-pending row surfacing alongside genuinely fresh ones). Durable rules: (1) for any advisory timeline claim, read the REPO advisory page (`/security/advisories/<GHSA>`) — its `published_at` is the vendor disclosure date; (2) a same-window feed cluster is evidence of one DB ingest wave, not coordinated disclosure; (3) batch-surfacing waves are discovery opportunities — everything in the wave is fresh to downstream consumers (Dependabot, OSV mirrors, scanners) simultaneously at ingest time, which is when the actual exposure clock starts for anyone relying on the feed.

**HEADLINE 2 — Vibe-Trading: a default-insecure AI-agent app at 34,446 GitHub stars (HKUDS/Vibe-Trading, "Your Personal Trading Agent", repo created Apr 1 2026).** Three GHSAs, PyPI package `vibe-trading-ai` >=0.1.0 <0.1.7, fixed v0.1.7 (commit `9454d4a`), all three imported into OSV at this wiki's check (no CVE on the lead, no GHSA alias beyond the trio):
- **`GHSA-v2f8-6655-7grj` (critical, CVSS 3.1 **10.0** scoped): unauthenticated full-API exposure → RCE chain.** The shipped `agent/.env.example` leaves `# API_AUTH_KEY=` COMMENTED OUT, and `require_auth()` executes `if not api_key: return` — unset key ⇒ every endpoint decorated with it operates unauthenticated; the shipped Dockerfile has NO `USER` directive (runs root); an unauthenticated file-write accepts `.py`/`.sh`/`.yaml` to a server-returned path; default-permissive CORS + a loopback-only check still grants any whitelisted-localhost browser page credentialed cross-origin access; `_mask_secret()` partially discloses API keys.
- **`GHSA-jqmf-mx4f-hfr6` (critical, 10.0): the LLM-callable tool registry is AUTO-DISCOVERED and UNCONDITIONAL in default config — no operator opt-in gates it**: `BashTool` shell-injection sink (the canonical RCE primitive), `BackgroundRunTool` async shell injection, backtest `exec_module()` executing top-level statements BEFORE the `SignalEngine` class check (a second independent RCE path that bypasses BashTool signatures), `read_url` outbound HTTP with no schema/host validation (SSRF), and Jinja2 codegen for `.py.j2` with autoescape OFF.
- **`GHSA-5rmq-chc7-m22f` (high, 7.5): file-read tools have no real sandbox** — `safe_user_path()` accepts anything under `Path.home()` or `Path.cwd()`, which inside the root container resolves to `/root` and `/app` (`/root/.ssh/id_rsa`, `/root/.aws/credentials`, `/root/.kube/config`, `/app/agent/.env` all pass), and `read_document()` has NO sandbox call at all: any path the process can read, including `/etc/shadow` and `/proc/self/environ`.
Durable reads: (1) this is the Unit 42 AgentCore lesson reproduced in open source — **the agent tool IS the perimeter**, and an auto-discovered tool registry with a live `BashTool` and no auth-by-default is the same default-on-root-shell failure, self-hosted by anyone who followed the tutorial; (2) "security that activates only when the operator sets a key" is no security — the insecure default is the shipped product; (3) two independent RCE paths (tool injection + `exec_module()` before the class check) means signature-hunting one primitive misses the other. Monitor: CVE assignment, ITW sightings of exposed `vibe-trading-ai` FastAPI surfaces, copycat defaults in other hobby-agent frameworks, how long the default-unauth state shipped before Jul 29 (repo does not say). Same ingest wave also carried: **Wasmtime `GHSA-2hw9-mc66-jc2q`** (preemption/traps during bulk ops break internal VM state, low), **`@fastify/busboy`** two DoS GHSAs (CVE-2026-19481 prototype-named part headers, CVE-2026-19484 oversized boundary), **sqlite3-ruby UAF** in aggregate arguments, **Xray-core** `pinnedPeerCertSha256` CA-pinning MITM success, **Praxis HTTP/2 Bomb**, **figlet/geopy/probe-image-size** DoS/ReDoS rows — all noted, below bar individually.

**HEADLINE 3 — Trigger.dev: 13 advisories surfaced in the same wave; the program is 37 GHSAs and the cluster grammar is a full tenant-boundary audit of an AI-agent background-jobs platform.** Selected with mechanism (fixed-version range, all patched ≤ 4.5.9):
- **`GHSA-9q4r-4842-93vw` (high, 7.7): cross-tenant SQLi in the TSQL query compiler** (`POST /api/v1/query`) — the compiler parameterizes/escapes ALL user input and injects a per-tenant `WHERE` guard — EXCEPT the **window-function name**, concatenated with no allowlist; a backtick-quoted identifier smuggles a raw subquery that sits OUTSIDE the tenant guard, reading any other organization's ClickHouse rows (verified end-to-end against a live ClickHouse). Patched 4.5.6. Lesson for every custom query compiler: the injection surface is the ONE field you forgot to parameterize — allowlist every concatenated identifier.
- **`GHSA-pqxw-g93w-hj9x` (high): self-hosted v4 default-secrets chain to full infrastructure compromise** — `hosting/docker/.env.example` SHIPS hardcoded `SESSION_SECRET`/`MAGIC_LINK_SECRET`/`ENCRYPTION_KEY`/`MANAGED_WORKER_SECRET`; the magic-link secret lets `remix-auth-email-link@2.0.2` forge auth tokens for ANY email with `validateSessionMagicLink` defaulting to `false`. **`GHSA-gg6r-gp4c-89hp` (critical): the `/coordinator` Socket.IO namespace authenticates with `"coordinator-secret"` baked into source, override variable undocumented anywhere (docs, .env.example, helm values)** — once connected, `READY_FOR_EXECUTION` returns any run's DECRYPTED env vars. Anyone who self-hosted without reading source is exposed.
- **The loader/action auth-split bug appears TWICE** (`GHSA-pp95-gc86-jq6q` run-replay POST has NO auth while the GET loader calls `requireUser` and org-scopes correctly; `GHSA-qxpp-qjg8-x4jv` replay authorizes the SOURCE run but takes the TARGET environment verbatim from the form → cross-tenant run injection) — a framework-level footgun (Remix exports two handlers per route file; only one of them got the auth wrapper). Plus: unauthenticated realtime-stream data injection via guessable `run_*` friendlyIds ("backwards compatibility" comment, no auth wrapper), two SSRF variants with ZERO private-IP/`169.254`/loopback filtering repo-wide (`GHSA-xxv7-2vv3-h682`, `GHSA-q567-cr4x-96w4`), cross-environment deployment cancel from a lower-trust dev/CI key, prototype pollution → cross-tenant, and **`GHSA-fj2x-mqqp-3v2w`: CLI `deploy --dry-run --log-level debug` prints every RESOLVED secret value in `options.envVars`** (the non-debug path correctly hides values) — anyone with the developer's terminal transcript or CI debug log holds the deployment secrets; fix 4.5.9. Note the July 9 program also includes a critical CLI auth-code-phishing → PAT takeover and an MCP-server advisory binding the default network interface (`GHSA-pqmg-rq46-vm4m`) — this platform's whole surface is agent-adjacent. The fifty-third sweep logged two of these GHSAs below bar at pair scale; at 13-in-one-wave / 37-program scale the cluster is captured; product vulns, no actor, still no page per wiki precedent. Monitor: self-hosted fleet uptake to 4.5.6/4.5.9, any cross-tenant abuse sighting, whether the loader/action auth-split draws a framework-level lint.

**Adjacent fix-residual rows in the wave:** **Composer `GHSA-96h3-5x6v-m776` / CVE-2026-59944** — the `GHSA-gjfg-22fp-rrxx` hardening is BYPASSED via symlinked package bin paths because the earlier fix only rejected literal `..` segments and only in one resolution location (a malicious package can chmod a file outside its own directory world-readable+executable on shared hosts); fixed 2.10.3 — same "reviewer audits the FIX" genre as the current SiYuan batches. **Dulwich `GHSA-8mcx-5rqc-vhmf` (8.8)** — Windows checkout validates `.git`/ADS/short-names/reserved devices but not **DOS drive-letter prefixes**: a tree entry literally named `C:` hijacks `os.path.join` out of the repo root = arbitrary file write; fixed 1.2.9.

**HEADLINE 4 — tl;dr sec #348 (Oct 1) lands directly on the Keygraph/Shannon agentic-validation watch:** featured post is **Google's PageBreak project — "agentic hacks, real proofs": Google's AI-powered web-app scanner paired with DETERMINISTIC VALIDATION** (plus a real-world-findings companion on bughunters.google.com) — i.e., Google shipping the exact design grammar this wiki tracks in Keygraph's Shannon: model finds, deterministic code proves. Issue #348 also carries Perplexity's **Numbat** (OSS agent-behavior detection/prevention across client endpoints, `perplexityai/numbat` + rules) and the NCSC's "One does not simply defend agentically"; issue #347 (Sep 24) carried "AI Agents Hacking Companies for $25" (threat actor using open-source attack harnesses). Monitor: PageBreak findings cadence + scope (does it extend beyond web apps), Numbat adoption, and whether deterministic-validation becomes the named industry pattern in vendor copy. → [source-index tl;dr sec entry](../notes/source-index.md)

**Gitea runner state change (patch-tracking re-key):** the CVE-2026-73802 advisory's package is `gitea.com/gitea/runner` — the old home is GONE: `github.com/go-gitea/act_runner` 404s and `proxy.golang.org` can no longer resolve the module (`git ls-remote` fails, repo prompts for credentials), so `go get`-over-HTTPS against the legacy path is dead — same removal shape as this wiki's Terraform-provider watch items. Releases now run on **gitea.com**: v4.0.0 Sep 24, v4.0.1 Sep 30, **v4.1.0 Oct 1 19:52Z (~27 h BEFORE the advisory published)**, v3.5.0 Sep 14. Commit `ddee2e05` (Oct 1, "align Docker actions, job env and host mode with GitHub", #1268) touches host-mode handling but is not confirmed as the `sanitizeConfig()` fix (patch endpoint requires login — could not diff at check). Monitor: whether v4.1.0 carries the `container.options` sanitization fix, self-hosted runner-fleet uptake, and the v4.x-vs-pseudo-version fix-claim mismatch (advisory range names a `1.0.9-0.2026...` pseudo-version the new release scheme has left behind).

## <a id="october-3-fifty-fifth-sweep"></a>October 3 fifty-fifth sweep (~05:00–05:40 UTC): OSV stream +1 — **`voxel-tts` = the kam193 `2026-10-voxeval` campaign PIVOTS NAME, version lineage continues across the pivot (0.4.5 → 0.5.0/0.5.1), PyPI already quarantine-tagged again within hours**; first on-wiki capture of the **`rmcp` (official MCP Rust SDK) OAuth-client SSRF** — a malicious/compromised MCP server turns the client's own OAuth discovery into a private-network fetch primitive (fixed 2.0.0) — the agent/MCP advisory genre the SiYuan batches opened now extends to the protocol SDK itself; global advisory feed has added NOTHING since the Oct 2 23:18Z ingest wave (fifty-fourth sweep's ingest-time read now has a clean baseline); all standing heartbeats quiet or holding

**OSV stream: MAL-2026-17461 `voxel-tts` (PyPI, source kam193, published 2026-10-03T04:38:14Z), 17462–17475 all 404 → HIGH-WATER NOW MAL-2026-17461, resume 17462.** Campaign field reads `2026-10-voxeval` — the SAME campaign tag as yesterday's `voxeval` discovery (MAL-2026-17457, versions 0.4.3–0.4.5) — but under a NEW package name: `voxel-tts` 0.5.0/0.5.1, "imitates real activity and instead deploys a coin miner" (kam193 text). The version numbers continue the dead name's lineage (0.4.5 quarantined → 0.5.0 live-on-paper), so the operator's version grammar spans the name pivot — **durable read: for delete-only campaigns the version counter is a cluster key that survives the name; hunt version-series continuity inside one campaign tag, not just name grammar.** Disposition at check: PyPI JSON-API 404 + `/simple/voxel-tts/` 200-empty carrying `pypi:project-status: quarantined` — the SAME hours-fast escalation `voxeval` reached and faster than dedh's ~2 days; zero GHSA alias (OSV query returns the MAL id with no aliases) = Dependabot-blind as its sibling. Neighboring names (`voxeltts`, `voxel-tts-py`, `real-voxel-tts`) carry zero OSV records = no re-upload/backfill wave yet. Monitor: GHSA mirror, re-registration of the quarantined shell's name, third name in the series, whether the miner payload is ever written up.

**First on-wiki: `rmcp` OAuth client fetches server-controlled `resource_metadata` URLs — GHSA-c9xm-49cp-xcr9 (published 2026-10-02T16:12Z, severity medium, fixed `rmcp` 2.0.0; repo `modelcontextprotocol/rust-sdk`, affected `main` at commit `c330fede`, file `crates/rmcp/src/transport/auth.rs`).** A malicious OR compromised MCP server answers the client's OAuth discovery with `401 WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/"` (or any localhost/RFC1918/cloud-metadata URL) and `rmcp` GETs it from the victim application's network context — `extract_www_authenticate_params()` accepts an absolute URL from the server-controlled header with NO same-origin, scheme, DNS-rebinding, or private-range check before `fetch_resource_metadata_from_url()` sends the request. Why this earns capture although medium-severity and CVE-less: (1) it is the **protocol SDK itself** — every Rust agent/MCP client built on `rmcp` inherits the primitive, the same "the agent tool IS the perimeter" lesson as the Vibe-Trading trio and Unit 42's AgentCore piece, now at the layer BELOW applications; (2) it completes a shape the SiYuan batches sketched from the other side — SiYuan was a malicious-payload-against-client SSRF bypass; `rmcp` is a default-trust-client-against-server SSRF, i.e. **OAuth discovery metadata is a server-authored redirect primitive** in any client that parses `WWW-Authenticate` naively. Durable hunt (no vendor needed): grep every MCP/OAuth client for `resource_metadata` parsing that lacks an origin allowlist + IP-range deny; Sigma/proxy rule: outbound GETs to link-local metadata hosts from agent processes carrying `MCP-Protocol-Version` headers. Monitor: whether other MCP SDKs (TS, Python) ship the same unvalidated fetch, CVE assignment, uptake of 2.0.0.

**Fifty-fourth-sweep mechanic pays off immediately:** the global advisory feed re-pulled at ~05:20Z shows **zero advisories published after the Oct 2 23:18:12Z row** — the ingest wave is closed, so the wave's contents (Vibe-Trading, Trigger.dev, SiYuan batch four, Gitea runner, A2UI) are now a bounded, captured set rather than a rolling one. ## <a id="october-4-seventy-fifth-sweep"></a>October 4 seventy-fifth sweep (~21:20–21:50 UTC): OSV stream +1 — **a SECOND live kam193 campaign appears on PyPI and it is not the cryptominer: `anthropic-sdk` 0.1.0 (MAL-2026-17472, campaign `2026-10-anthropic-sdk`) is an import-time INFOSTEALER impersonating the Anthropic SDK itself, and this wiki pulled and read the full payload while it was still serving — harvest list scoped exactly to the AI-developer estate (ANTHROPIC/OPENAI keys, `.claude.json`, `.mcp.json`, aider chat history, gh tokens), staging on the public jsDelivr GitHub-proxy rail, DNS-TXT exfil fallback, and a central DNS-TXT kill switch; the PyPI name was quarantined within the hour but the GitHub repo and BOTH CDN copies of the payload were still LIVE at check** ; all standing heartbeats quiet or holding

- **HEADLINE — the harvest list is the targeting statement.** This wiki fetched the full 20,852-byte payload (`66a6f755522497c1bd7dc95e2d24a1550770d50c34387d3af3d4bc94418a62ee`) from BOTH `raw.githubusercontent.com/shred0day/payload/main/payload.py` and `cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/shred0day/payload@main/payload.py` — byte-identical, both HTTP 200 at check. Docstring: "Payload module for anthropic-sdk update check. Collects local install stats and reports them. All paths fail silently." Every sensitive string is rebuilt at runtime from char-code arrays (the same `fromCharCode` lineage this page documented on the npm side, now in Python). The credential-frAGMENT set it assembles covers cloud+CI+package tokens (`AWS_`, `GITHUB_`, `GITLAB_`, `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `API_`, `PRIVATE`, `SSH_`, `SYNK_` (Snyk), `OSSF_`, `CI`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITLAB_CI`, `JENKINS_HOME`) plus the AI-specific estate: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPICTOKEN`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAITOKEN`, `github-copilot`, `.claude.json`, `.mcp.json`/`mcp.json`, `.aider.chat.history.md`, `ChatGPT`, `LM Studio`, `.open-interpreter`, `gho_`/`ghp_`/`ghs_` token prefixes, `.git-credentials`, AWS creds files, `id_rsa`/`id_ed25519`. **Read: this is not a spray-and-take stealer — the collection set is scoped to exactly what an AI-agent developer's home directory contains, and the impersonated product is the SDK those developers install.** Same-week cross-reference on-wiki: the Zammad agent-breach page (agent tooling as target and as tool) and the rmcp/MCP client-trust line — the AI-developer workstation is now a named, targeted credential estate in the wild.
- **Kill switch + exfil grammar.** Execution path: import fires a daemon thread → 5–30 s random delay → `env_score() >= 3` aborts on sandbox signals (VMware `00:05:69`/`00:0c:29`/`00:50:56`, VirtualBox `00:1c:14`, Parallels `08:00:27` MACs, `cuckoo`/`detonate`/`\bvm\b` hostname regex, `C:\sandbox`, `C:\analysis`) → a 24-h per-host stamp file de-duplicates repeats → **`check_hold()` reads TXT at `_ks.telemetry-edge.net` and aborts if the value starts with `1`/`on` = a central DNS-based execution switch the operator can flip for the entire infected population without touching the payload**. ## <a id="october-5-eighty-second-sweep"></a>October 5 eighty-second sweep (~15:15–15:50 UTC): OSV +1 = `MAL-2026-17571` — and it is NOT stream noise: **`@subql/common@5.8.3`, a legitimate web3-infra project's release pipeline turned into an artifact-substitution backdoor** (StepSecurity disclosure, same-hour GHSA mirror) → **[new page: SubQuery release-mirror compromise](subql-common-ci-artifact-substitution-release-pipeline-compromise-october-2026.md)**

## <a id="october-6-ninetieth-sweep"></a>October 6 ninetieth sweep (~09:25–10:10 UTC): OSV QUIET (high-water holds 17636) — a sweep spent proving the wiki's own probes: the eighty-ninth's bucket-clock table RE-VERIFIED unchanged to the second against the canonical `ltidi.storage.googleapis.com/depenconf/` host (all five loader objects 200, counters 403, no wave-5), and this sweep's FIRST-pass probe strings were twice wrong in instructive ways — a mis-hosted bucket URL returned `NoSuchBucket` forever on a fully-live bucket, and a `/messages`-suffix typo on four host names returned four honest `Not found`s that briefly read as "npm ENFORCED." Corrected ground truth: **all five GHSAd ltidisafe hosts STILL LIVE, ZERO registry action eleven days after the first GHSA.** Real state-change this sweep: **RubyGems completed its death ladder on the last 44 yanked shells — every sampled `.gem` tarball 403, compact index empty-versioned account-wide, and the three advisory-blind ANCHOR names (`reqthrottle_3474`, `reqthrottle_mini`, `eth-wallet-tools`) now BARE-404 fully erased — while still carrying ZERO OSV + ZERO GHSA: the fleet dies at 42/48 advisory coverage, anchor invisible to every lane to the end.**

**OSV: QUIET — 17637–17646 sequential 404, re-check 17637/17647/17657/17667 404, two checks. HIGH-WATER HOLDS MAL-2026-17636, RESUME 17637.**

**(1) SELF-CORRECTION — PROBE-STRING DISCIPLINE (the eighty-ninth's bucket-clock section is VALID and fully re-verified; THIS sweep's first probe string was wrong).** The sweep's first bucket-probe pass used `storage.googleapis.com/depenconf/ltidisafe-<v>.tgz` — dropping the `ltidi.` BUCKET HOST and leaving only the path segment — and GCS answered `NoSuchBucket` at every version, which briefly read as "the whole bucket is gone." Ground truth, re-pulled this sweep against the canonical dependency host `https://ltidi.storage.googleapis.com/depenconf/`: **3.7.8 (1,945 B), 3.7.9 (1,949 B), 3.8.1 (1,945 B), 3.8.2 (1,948 B), 3.8.3 (1,946 B, sha `5df74769…` byte-identical re-verified) ALL 200 with the SAME `Last-Modified` stamps the eighty-ninth recorded (Sep 25 04:33:16Z / Oct 2 18:05:52Z / 18:08:02Z / 18:09:04Z / Oct 5 16:05:37Z) — the bucket-clock table stands, unchanged to the second; `3.8.4`/`3.9.0`/`4.0.0` 403 = counter unmoved, no wave-5, no re-upload/re-arm on any serving object.** No bucket was deleted: `depenconf` is the path segment inside the `ltidi` bucket, not a bucket name (the fleet's "twin" is the npm loader name `ltidiconf`, a different object entirely). The mechanic lesson is general and sharp: **GCS answers a nonexistent bucket with `NoSuchBucket` at EVERY object path — bucket-level 404s are indistinguishable from object-level 404s by status code alone, so a mis-hosted probe string returns "404" forever on a fully-live campaign and looks EXACTLY like "counter not moving." Any counter-probe watch must read the XML `<Code>` at least once (403 ACL-denied vs 404-no-bucket vs object-absent are three different states) and diff the probe string against the canonical dependency URI.** Distribution state: one bucket, serving unbroken May→Oct, re-confirmed live end-to-end this sweep.

**(2) FALSE-POSITIVE CAUGHT IN FLIGHT — THE "HOSTS GONE" READING WAS THIS WIKI'S OWN TYPO.** The sweep's first host-inventory pass queried `@airbnb-extended/messages`, `@smwebserver/messages`, `@risk-detection/messages`, `@unified-platform/messages` — inventing a `/messages` suffix on names that are actually `@airbnb-extended/typescript-config`, `@smwebserver/static`, and the bare `risk-detection`/`unified-platform`. npm answered four honest `{"error":"Not found"}` and the sweep briefly wrote up an "npm ENFORCED, four hosts deleted" state-change. Re-probing the CANONICAL names ~5 min later: **ALL FIVE HOSTS STILL LIVE — every doc 200 with non-empty `99.9.1` versions, `@pinecone-experience/messages` tarball re-pulled byte-identical `f6acde00…`, all four unscoped/second-lane manifests re-read with deps still pinning their bucket URLs (`typescript-config`→3.7.8, `static`→3.7.9, `risk-detection`→3.8.1, `unified-platform`→3.8.2), `whltd1` maintainer query still returns all five names, download counters unchanged (423/414/277/188/wk). Enforcement ledger unchanged: ELEVEN days after the first GHSA, ZERO registry action, ZERO holders.** The durable read flips to method, not registry: **a name-probe 404 is only evidence when the probe string is copied from the canonical record — "Not found" is the sound the registry makes for typos and takedowns alike.** The eighty-ninth's standing line holds verbatim: advisory clock hours, registry-action clock weeks, still running. (`@airbnb-extended/messages`/`@smwebserver/messages`/`@risk-detection/messages`/`@unified-platform/messages` return Not found at check and are UNCLAIMED — free squat surface if the campaign wants lookalike reinfection names; new hunt-string watch.)

**(3) RUBYGEMS ERASURE DEEPENED PAST THE EIGHTY-SECOND'S LADDER FLOOR — THE HTML SOFT-PRESERVATION LAYER DIED ON THE ANCHOR NAMES.** The eighty-second measured the three-layer ladder (yank → API erasure → index rebuild + CDN 403) and recorded that **the HTML page `/gems/<name>` keeps serving 200** even after all three layers complete. The surviving `0.0.0-stage` stub (tgz sha256 `7d5d8596…`, `{"stub": true}`) carries *`Temporary package placeholder for staged publishing`* **word-for-word = SIGHTING SEVEN of the staging grammar** (online-header → smwebserver → dzyclutch → documenclient/serpacksven → hardhat-init → captchetat → here), first sighting inside a Baileys-family name.

**(3) THE STALE-SCOPE BLIND SPOT, CONFIRMED IN PRODUCTION.** `MAL-2026-17363` (published Sep 30 09:57Z, amazon-inspector, references OX Security's PhantomSub writeup) covers **`0.0.5` ONLY** — yet the account shipped `0.0.6`/`0.0.7`/`0.0.8` over the following week, `latest` moving through versions NO advisory described, with the downloads API showing 1,320 pulls Sep 27–Oct 5. Same grammar as the zencleaner 1.0.4 and MALFEX `cdn-img-fetch` scope breaks, but WORSE: those were un-advised live versions on names with partial coverage; this is a live iteration chain running one step ahead of a published record for five days. Post-purge at 15:45:09Z the OSV record gained the GHSA alias (mirrored ~66 min after GHSA publish — second data point on the GHSA→OSV import clock, 63 min then 66 min) but **still lists only `0.0.5`** = the import reflected provenance, not scope. Canonical registry docs remain ground truth: **all five ltidisafe hosts still live at day-11/4/1 (docs 200, maintainers `whltd1`, `latest` 99.9.1, `@airbnb-extended/typescript-config` tarball 200); ZERO enforcement.**

## <a id="october-6-ninety-fourth-sweep"></a>October 6 ninety-fourth sweep (~16:40–17:25 UTC): THE COLLECTOR BODY IS AN ACCOUNT FINGERPRINT — every armed oastify response echoes a FIXED PREFIX shared across that collector-account's tokens, and this wiki's eight canonical tokens cluster into exactly THREE Burp Collaborator origins: all five ltidisafe wave tokens (Sep 25 → Oct 5, three waves) = ONE account, the Wix wave-3 collector = ANOTHER, the two one-off-squat collectors = a THIRD — token provenance without any operator secret. And the curated lane's first RETROACTIVE SCOPE REVISIONS arrived: PR #1598 rewrites FOUR four-year-old/one-year-old records from blanket `introduced:0` ranges to explicit version lists that INCLUDE the `0.0.1-security` holder version itself — while still unmerged, hence invisible to every OSV consumer. HIGH-WATER HOLDS `MAL-2026-17637`, resume 17638 (17638–17642 404 two passes)

**(1) OSV: QUIET.** Sequential 17638–17642 + carry-over spots 404 across two passes; no stream movement since 17637.

**(2) HEADLINE — ARMED-COLLECTOR BODIES ARE DETERMINISTIC PER ACCOUNT: shared prefix = same Collaborator origin.** Re-probing the eight canonical tokens under the ninety-third's body-size rule (GET, body ≠ 1,190 B catch-all), every armed body is a 55/56-B page containing a token-derived string — and the strings are NOT independent: all five ltidisafe per-wave tokens (`1vxhqpj0…` 3.7.8/Sep 25, `fl4vg39e…` 3.7.9, `ykmefm8x…` 3.8.1, `bapr5zya…` 3.8.2, `1y0htpm0…` 3.8.3/Oct 5) echo bodies sharing the identical 23-character prefix `8n0l3yjy9840z4tan9r91vz` with only the tail varying per token; Wix wave-3 `unl9pgk6…` echoes a different prefix (`7uznim4i…`); the two one-off-squat collectors `187d714jo62z…`/`9q0lp9mr6ek7…` share a THIRD prefix (`4rgpacf6…`). Two passes ~3 min apart returned byte-identical bodies = deterministic, not session-random. Read: **the armed body is a fingerprint of the collector ACCOUNT (or its master secret), not of the individual token — two tokens with the same body prefix provably came from the same Burp Collaborator server/account even when their strings share no visible relationship.** Consequences: (a) the ltidisafe campaign's three loader waves across eleven days are cryptographically pinned to one attacker account — the strongest same-operator evidence this fleet has, and it was obtained from unauthenticated GETs; (b) new durable hunt mechanic: for any NEW `*.oastify.com` sighting, GET it, and if the body prefix matches one of the three known prefixes you have instant cluster attribution before reading a single payload; (c) the three prefixes now on the IoC line below are themselves hunt keys; (d) caveat recorded: this measures the Collaborator hosting, not the operator's identity — a shared/reused server account could in principle host distinct operators, though nothing here suggests it. The fabricated-token control still returns the 1,190-B stock page (re-run this sweep, catch-all confirmed unchanged).

**(3) CURATED LANE — FIRST RETROACTIVE SCOPE REVISIONS, AND THEY TRAVEL THROUGH MERGE LATENCY.** `ossf/malicious-packages` **PR #1598** (reporter `anthony-tho`, opened 15:40:10Z, still OPEN at check) *"Scoping Multiple Epic Games Packages"* rewrites four pre-existing OSV records: `MAL-2022-2806` (`eslint-config-support`, published **2022** — four years stale), `MAL-2025-47990` (`e2e-test-utils`), `MAL-2025-49241` (`store-webpack-config`), `MAL-2025-49244` (`windows_registry_editor`, PyPI-era file now carrying npm-style version lists). The edit pattern is uniform and double-edged: the blanket `introduced: 0` range is REMOVED and replaced with an explicit `versions` list — which (a) ADDS the never-malicious `0.0.1-security` security-holder version to the affected list (tooling noise: scanners will now flag npm's own placeholder as malware-covered), and (b) NARROWS what the blanket range covered, the same stale-scope exposure as `17363` but in reverse — a future re-publish at any unlisted version slips out of scope. `windows_registry_editor` is 404 on PyPI at check; the three npm names are holder-only (`0.0.1-security` sole version) = all four names fully neutralized, which is precisely WHEN the curated lane is revisiting them. ## <a id="october-7-ninety-eighth-sweep"></a>October 7 ninety-eighth sweep (~03:25–03:45 UTC): THE CONTROL-PROBE RULE EXPANDS TO THE ADVISORY API ITSELF — this sweep's first OSV walk ran on `api.osv.dev/vulns/…` WITHOUT the `/v1/` prefix and got route-level 404s for EVERY id including known-live records; the walk that "proved the stream quiet" was itself blind, caught only because a `v1/query` cross-check on `css-jptvix-polyfill` returned `MAL-2026-17638` alive. SECOND HEADLINE: THE OSCILLATION MODEL COMPLETES ITS FIRST FULL CYCLE AND THE WHOLE COLLECTOR SET GOES DARK — `unl9pgk6…` ran armed → dark → armed → dark in ten hours, and for the first time at check EVERY probed token across BOTH campaigns (Wix pair + ltidisafe day-11 first-wave + one-off pair) sits at the 1,190-B catch-all

**(1) OSV: QUIET — on the corrected route. HIGH-WATER HOLDS `MAL-2026-17639`, RESUME 17640.** The first-pass walk of 17640–17650 used `https://api.osv.dev/vulns/MAL-2026-…` (no `/v1/`). That path is not an API route: it answers **404 for every id**, known-live controls included (`17638`/`17639`/`17636` all "404"), with body `{"code":404,"message":"The current request is not defined by this API."}` (73 B) — a ROUTE error — while the correct `https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/…` answers 200 for live records and 404 with body `{"code":5,"message":"Vulnerability not found"}` (46 B) for absent ones. Same status code, two meanings, body-only discrimination. The self-catch was cross-lane, not intra-lane: the sweep's `v1/query` name-lookups (which use a different endpoint that never broke) returned `css-jptvix-polyfill` → `MAL-2026-17638` live while the bad-route walk called the same record absent — the exact contradiction that flagged the probe, same class as the ninetieth's dropped-bucket-host GCS probe and the ninety-third's oastify catch-all. **Durable mechanics: (a) a quiet-stream claim REQUIRES a known-live control id inside the same walk and route — a stream walk without a control is an unmeasured walk; (b) for OSV, read the 404 BODY, not the status: `code:5 "Vulnerability not found"` = absent record, `code:404 "not defined by this API"` = wrong route (and the wiki's high-water bookkeeping is unaffected this time only because prior sweeps' finds were independently corroborated by query-lane pulls).** SECOND durable mechanic, confirmed the same window: **OSV direct GET does NOT resolve GHSA aliases** — `GET /v1/vulns/GHSA-m6m7-6f4x-c5v3` and `/GHSA-367w-p5mp-qr5j` both 404 while GitHub's own `GET /advisories/GHSA-m6m7-6f4x-c5v3` is 200 and the OSV records are live under their `MAL-` primaries with those aliases inside. For malware-lane advisories the GHSA id is alias-only at OSV: resolve by `MAL-` primary, by `v1/query` package lookup, or at GitHub — never declare a malware GHSA "not in OSV" from a direct-id 404. Corrected-route re-walk of 17637–17650: 17637/17638/17639 200, 17640–17650 404 (record-not-found body) = stream genuinely quiet this window, high-water holds.

**(2) HEADLINE — THE COLLECTOR SET GOES FLEET-WIDE DARK; the ninety-seventh's session-oscillation model completes its first full cycle.** Re-GETs at ~03:3xZ (two passes ~45 s apart), all against the body-size rule: **`unl9pgk6…` (Wix wave-3) 1,190-B catch-all — DARK, `orj3tao0…` (Wix wave-4) DARK, `1vxhqpj0…` (ltidisafe Sep-25 first-wave, ARMED continuously since the ninetieth at day 11) DARK, `187d714jo62z…` (one-off squat pair) DARK (no re-arm flip since the ninety-seventh).** For the first time since the fingerprint hunt began, ZERO of the probed canonical tokens across BOTH campaigns is armed at check. ## <a id="october-8-one-hundred-twelfth-sweep"></a>October 8 one-hundred-and-twelfth sweep (~09:0x–09:3x UTC): THE MACHINE LANE RESTARTS WITH `MAL-2026-17668` = `@dransay/address-validation` — A SELF-DECLARED "BENIGN BUG-BOUNTY BEACON" (DrAnsay/YesWeHack report #YWH-PGM42275-93, researcher `0xamino_hunter`) FLAGGED MALWARE-CLASS IN ≈46 MIN — the THIRD authorized-canary collision on this page and the cleanest test yet of the Sep-20 rule "a canary claim is unfalsifiable from the artifact"; HIGH-WATER 17668 RESUME 17669; FLEET: `8n0l3yjy…` ~28 h, `zdz5vdmv…` second consecutive check, wave-1 inversion ENDS into a both-dark phase

This is ALSO the machine lane's first single-record tick since the 90-minute drain — the backlog is gone and the stream is back to fresh-name cadence.

**(2) THIS-WIKI TARBALL FORENSICS (live pull, `sha256 5d6846c4…bbc15`):** three files total — `package.json` fires `preinstall: node beacon.js`; `beacon.js` does EXACTLY two network acts: a DNS `lookup('address-validation.db3klhbi6i9hark1kegg174t38h33b6wt.oast.site')` and one HTTPS `GET` to the same host, path = package name, 2.5-s fail-open timeout; no env, no files, no system info, no persistence — README claims publication "at the explicit request of the DrAnsay / YesWeHack triage team (report #YWH-PGM42275-93)" by researcher `0xamino_hunter`, to test whether DrAnsay CI resolves internal `@dransay/*` names against public npm, and offers name transfer on request. **The `.oast.site` collector is LIVE and answering:** direct GET at ~09:2xZ returned a 72-B body `tw6b33h83t471ggek1krah9i6ibhlk3bd` (deterministic across two probe paths; the FIRST `.oast.site`-lane collector body on this wiki — the fleet's other lanes are `.oastify.com` and `.oast.fun`, same PortSwigger family). Registry clock: scope created Sep 29 (`0.0.1` placeholder), malicious-class `99.0.0` published **08:00:14Z**, OpenSSF verdict 08:46:02Z ≈ 46 min, name LIVE with `latest = 99.0.0` (flagged version) at check, 115 dl/wk. **No GHSA mirror yet** (~1 h after flag; the mirror-dedupe +1–2.5 h clock from last sweep predicts one within the hour).

**(3) THE PATTERN, NAMED: bug-bounty canaries keep landing on the public registry and the machine lane flags them all.** This page has now counted THREE: Sep 18 wave (five names, real malware per behavior), Sep 20 `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` (T-Bank bounty claim — flagged malware-class six minutes after publish while claiming authorization), and now `@dransay/address-validation` (YesWeHack claim — flagged in 46 min). The Sep-20 rule applies verbatim and gains its second confirmation: **a canary claim is unfalsifiable from the artifact** — passive beacon to operator-controlled infrastructure is byte-for-byte the same shape as an attack's first act, and the only difference is the publisher's own README. Note the ASYMMETRY the machine lane creates: a defender's authorized canary gets flagged malware-class just as fast as real malware (good for coverage, bad for defenders who want their own beacons to survive — pin `@dransay:registry=<private>` and treat ANY public-scope hit as either a confused deputy or a claim you must verify out-of-band), while a malware author can copy the canary costume verbatim and get the same "recon-looking" artifact with zero benefit of the doubt. The `@dransay` beacon is measurably gentler than the T-Bank one (DNS+GET of a package name vs the v99.0.1 full git-remote/CI-var harvest) — the gentleness changes exposure, not triage: OpenSSF does not and cannot intake-verify permission claims, and neither should scanner consumers. Monitor: DrAnsay/YesWeHack-side confirmation, name transfer to DrAnsay (the README offers it — a `0.0.1-security`-style holder event would complete a fourth enforcement shape), GHSA mirror arrival, `0xamino_hunter`-pattern follow-on names (`address-verification`/`email-validation`/`phone-validation`/`sso-helper` all 404 at check = single-name test so far).

**(4) ALIASES-LAG ALERT EXTENDED:** `MAL-2026-17665/17666/17667` (the Tailwind trio) STILL read `aliases: None` at ~09:0xZ = **≥2.5 h after** `GHSA-v2r3`/`GHSA-vx3q`/`GHSA-p4jm` went public — the lazy-join batch clock is not minutes-scale; name-keyed dual-lane queries remain the only reliable coverage check.

## <a id="october-8-one-hundred-thirteenth-sweep"></a>October 8 one-hundred-and-thirteenth sweep (~11:2x–11:4x UTC): THE CURATED LANE DISCLOSES THE WHOLE SCOPE THE MACHINE LANES MISSED 7 OF 8 — `ossf/malicious-packages` PR #1606 (InvisiRisk / `ir-pranesh-shrestha`, 08:26:10Z) files ALL EIGHT `@dransay` canary names as malicious: same publisher `0xamino_hunter`, six-minute publish window 07:56:51–08:02:19Z, identical `preinstall: node beacon.js` → SAME `.oast.site` collector, per-name URL paths — seven names LIVE with ZERO OSV + ZERO GHSA = the 46-min flag last sweep was the machine lane's ONE-name hit inside an eight-name scope; `GHSA-rvr3-j766-6jcp` (`critical`) lands on `address-validation` at 09:31:57Z ≈45 min after the OSV record, aliases join STILL absent on `17665`–`17668` ≥3 h post-GHSA; FLEET: ALL SEVEN tracked account prefixes ARMED at one check — `8n0l3yjy…` ≈30 h, wave-1 squat spellings re-ARMED with account bodies while account spellings sit catch-all (inversion, second time), personio `.oast.fun` lane answers armed for the FIRST time on this wiki; HIGH-WATER HOLDS 17668 RESUME 17669 (17669–17674 HTTP-404 at final read, fifteenth control sweep)

No fresh machine-lane mints since 08:46:02Z despite the eight-name scope being disclosed at 08:26Z — see (2), which is the sweep's actual headline.

**(2) THE `@dransay` SCOPE IS EIGHT NAMES, NOT ONE — and the machine lanes flagged exactly one.** Curated PR #1606 (opened 08:26:10Z, reporter account `ir-pranesh-shrestha`, files labeled `MAL-0000-invisirisk-*` = InvisiRisk, the same reporter behind #1564 dirtyblanket, #1571 amel10, #1578 @bluewin/utils, #1585/#1586, #1591 ph-common, #1592 testneo7755, #1601 personio — nine curated filings since Sep 29) files ALL EIGHT names published by `0xamino_hunter` on 2026-10-08 between **07:56:51Z and 08:02:19Z — a 5.5-minute scripted window**: `@dransay/db`, `@dransay/secrets`, `@dransay/api`, `@dransay/phone-fix-test`, `@dransay/feature-flags`, `@dransay/logger`, `@dransay/address-validation`, unscoped `dransay`, all `= 99.0.0`, each with `preinstall: node beacon.js` making the DNS lookup + HTTPS GET of the package name to the SAME collector `db3klhbi6i9hark1kegg174t38h33b6wt.oast.site`, path-per-name (`/db`, `/secrets`, `/api`, `/phone-fix-test`, `/feature-flags`, `/logger`, `/address-validation`, `/dransay-unscoped`). **Registry verification by this wiki at ~11:3xZ: all EIGHT names LIVE with `latest = 99.0.0` = the flagged version, every doc's single maintainer `0xamino_hunter`, no holder events.** The collector re-probed LIVE this sweep (`GET /db` → 72-B body `tw6b33h8…`, byte-identical). The OSV machine lane (OpenSSF Package Analysis) minted ONLY `MAL-2026-17668` for `address-validation`; GHSA malware lane only `GHSA-rvr3-j766-6jcp` for the same one name. **Seven names — including the deliciously baited `@dransay/secrets` and the scope-root `dransay` — are installable right now with zero public advisory on either machine lane.** This inverts last sweep's framing twice over: (a) the canary "single-name test so far" read (the four 404 names probed were simply not this researcher's chosen names) was WRONG — the test was an eight-name scope from the start, published BEFORE the sweep that called it single-name; (b) the durable asymmetry statement gets its sharper form: **the machine lane did not flag authorized canaries "as fast as malware" — it flagged ONE name of an eight-name authorized-canary scope and left seven installable, while a real malware campaign of the same size published in one window would have tripped per-name analysis on each name within ~46 min if its beacon domain had been classified first.** The differentiator is not speed per name — it is scope: the machine lane analyzed `address-validation` because its beacon domain hit a malicious-activity verdict; the seven siblings beaconing the SAME domain were never individually adjudicated because Package Analysis scores per-package and its sandbox verdict stream only picked one of eight (or the other seven failed to trip the same heuristic — the beacon content is byte-similar per the PR's evidence section). Either way the coverage lesson for scanner consumers is the SIBLING RULE: **when any one name in a scope gets flagged, immediately query and quarantine every sibling name under the same publisher/scope/timestamp window — do not wait for per-name advisories.** InvisiRisk's per-name "flagged by" timestamps (07:56:57–08:02:30Z, seconds after each publish) show a third party was watching this publication stream LIVE as it happened — the fastest on-record human observation clock this page has (5–18 s per name vs OpenSSF's 46 min for the one record). Whether npm takes any action on the seven LIVE names before this PR merges is the abuse-lane test to watch; monitor: PR #1606 merge → eight OSV records mint (retro-wave pattern), whether the other seven get GHSAs, holder/`0.0.1-security` events on any of the eight, collector body persistence, and whether the DrAnsay/YesWeHack attribution survives the scope disclosure intact.
